## What the commit messages show

Taken from commit subjects only. No outcomes are claimed beyond what the messages say.

- **Features:** initial support for yielding objects (2013-11); passing context and
  arguments through thunks; support for regular functions that throw; a better error

  wrapping results in `Promise.resolve()`.

- **Maintenance:** README and History.md updates, removal of unused code and an unused
  dependency, dependency updates.

- **Review and merging:** 12 merge commits of external pull requests (2014-01 to 2014-10).

# Contribution summary from git history

**Author identity:** Jonathan Ong <<email:d29caa5c>> (the git identity configured in the repository)
**Period of commits:** 2013-11-07 to 2015-07-09
**Source:** `git log` on the repository, generated 2026-10-22. No source code is included.

## Totals for this identity

| Measure | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Commits authored | 68 (299 in the whole history) |
| Non-merge commits | 56 |
| Merge commits (merging other people's pull requests) | 12 |
| Lines added / removed (non-merge commits) | 1,151 / 1,075 |
| Other main author (by name, non-merge commits) | TJ Holowaychuk, 90; this author's name appears on 60 |

Project: a JavaScript library for generator-based asynchronous control flow (npm package `co`, versions 3.0.1 to 4.6.0 appear in this identity's commits).

## What the commits show

Descriptions below restate commit messages. They make no claim about adoption or measured impact.

**Core library changes**
- Added initial support for yielding objects (2013-11-07).
- Fixed "Generator is already running" errors when no callback is provided, with a test case (2013-11-24).
- Passed context and arguments through thunks; fixed a callback passed as an argument to generators (2013-11 to 2013-12).
- Improved the error message when a non-yieldable value is yielded; preserved key ordering for objects (2014-06-30).
- Switched the implementation to promises, and always wrapped results in `Promise.resolve()` (2014-10-27, 2014-12-18).
- Added support for regular functions that throw (2015-03-17).
- Added support for passing the remaining arguments of `co` to the generator (version 4.6.0, 2015-07-09).

**Releases**
- Release commits for 3.0.1, 3.0.2, 3.0.3, 3.1.0, 4.0.1, 4.0.2, 4.1.0, 4.2.0, 4.3.0, 4.3.1, 4.4.0, 4.5.0, 4.5.1, 4.5.2 and 4.6.0, with History.md updates.

**Testing and tooling**
- Added test coverage reporting and a Travis CI configuration; added testing on io.js (2014-10 to 2015-02).
- Added recursion, object and regression tests; removed an unused dependency (2013-11 to 2014-12).
- Added and later removed benchmarks (2013-11 to 2014-10).

**Documentation and packaging**
- Readme edits, badges, platform compatibility notes, and a `component.json` (2013-11 to 2014-10).

**Maintenance**
- 12 merge commits integrating pull requests from outside contributors.

Full per-commit data (hash, date, subject, files changed, lines added and removed) is in `commit-log.csv`.

## How to read this document

- Counts cover only the email address above. Other addresses in the history (for example <email:4132b6bd>) are not included.
- Git author names and emails are written by whoever makes the commit, so this document shows what the history records, not independent proof of identity. A reviewer can check the commit hashes against the repository if access is arranged.
- Line counts measure change volume, not effort or quality.

If the "private client engagement" description is wrong or only partly right, fix it before submitting. I did not write any claim about the client or the repo's privacy.

## Work areas (from commit subjects)
- Generator and thunk handling: fixes for "Generator is already running", passing context and arguments through thunks, and callback handling.
- Object yielding: initial `yield object` support, key-order preservation, clearer errors for non-yieldable values.
- Move to a promise-based implementation (4.0), including always wrapping results in `Promise.resolve()`, and the switch from Q to bluebird.
- Tooling: Travis and io.js CI, test coverage, benchmarks, `component.json`.
- Documentation, History.md and README upkeep, and reviewing and merging community pull requests.

They made 63 of the 131 changes recorded in that period (48%), and 697 of the 1,417 lines of code still in the project today are theirs (49%). They were active in 24 of the 94 weeks in that period. 42 people wrote code in this project; by share of surviving lines they rank 1. In that period they added 1,276 lines and deleted 1,762 lines, counted after the removals described next. 13 merge commits by them (4117a48, 70ba8e2, c8b76ae, 023ecb4, aa59ffd, e42133e, 9aef83e, 8413e5d, 9cdbfeb, 50ed090, 7ad53ac, b47f068, 1bb08e7) were not counted. - bot commits (not counted): 0
- excluded paths the author touched: 0 (0 lines added, 0 deleted)

### In plain language

Jonathan Ong worked in fx from 7 November 2013 to 22 August 2015, a span of 654 days. They made 63 of the 131 changes recorded in that period (48%), and 697 of the 1,417 lines of code still in the project today are theirs (49%). The second number is the one that lasts, and here it is the higher of the two: their work survived everything written since better than the count of changes suggests. 10 of the 19 files in the project were started by them (53%). They were active in 24 of the 94 weeks in that period. 42 people wrote code in this project; by share of surviving lines they rank 1. In that period they added 1,276 lines and deleted 1,762 lines, counted after the removals described next. 13 merge commits by them (4117a48, 70ba8e2, c8b76ae, 023ecb4, aa59ffd, e42133e, 9aef83e, 8413e5d, 9cdbfeb, 50ed090, 7ad53ac, b47f068, 1bb08e7) were not counted. Before any of this was counted, 0 automated changes and 0 machine-written or copied files were removed, so none of them inflate the figures. ### Tenure window

2013-11-07 to 2015-08-22 (654 days)

How: `git log --format=%aI --author=<identity>; first and last commit dates`

What this cannot show: Tenure is measured from commits, so work before the first commit or after the last is invisible. The first and last days are read in the offset each commit records, so they are the author's calendar days rather than UTC days.

### Share of commits in tenure

63 of 131 non-merge commits, 48.1%

How: `git log --no-merges --format=%aE --since=<first> --until=<last>; count by author over all`

What this cannot show: Commits measure activity, not what survived. One commit can be a typo or a subsystem. Squash-merged branches count once regardless of size.

### Cadence

24 active weeks of 94, 2.6 commits per active week, longest streak 3 weeks
17 of 23 release tags in tenure

How: `git log --no-merges --format=%aI --author=<identity>; ISO weeks in the author's own offset; git for-each-ref refs/tags with creatordate`

What this cannot show: A week with one commit and a week with forty both count as active. Tags are releases only if the project tags releases. Weeks are ISO weeks read in the offset each commit records, so they are the author's weeks; a commit made Monday morning in Auckland counts as Monday, not as the previous week.

### Test-file changes and documents created

What this cannot show: Test-file changes are counted, not test cases or coverage. A one-line README and a design document count the same; a document rewritten by someone else stays with its creator.

### Files authored

10 of 19 files alive at HEAD, 52.6% (degree of authorship)

How: `git log --no-merges --numstat -M over the history read; DOA = 3.293 + 1.098*FA + 0.164*DL - 0.321*ln(1 + AC) per file and author (Avelino et al.); authored when DOA is at least 3.293 and above 75% of the file's maximum; over files alive at HEAD`

What this cannot show: The coefficients were fitted on other systems; first authorship outweighs later rewrites, so a file rewritten from scratch by someone else can stay with its creator. A file that was renamed carries its history only when git detected the rename.

### Major-contributor components

major contributor in 2 of 2 directories (at least 5% of commits)

How: `git log --no-merges --numstat; directories at depth 2 where the author's share of commits touching them is at least 5% (Bird et al.)`

What this cannot show: Commit exposure treats a typo and a subsystem alike; five percent of the commits to a directory is a low bar by design.

### Commit size

How: `git log --no-merges --numstat --author=<identity>; added plus deleted lines per commit over included files; median and 90th percentile by nearest rank; huge counts commits over 10,000 lines`

What this cannot show: Size is not value. Imports, vendoring that slipped past the exclusions and reformat commits dominate the 90th percentile.

## What the commits did (from commit messages)

- **Core behaviour:** added yield-object support (2013-11); fixed "Generator is already running" errors (2013-11); passed context and arguments through thunks; preserved key ordering; supported regular functions that throw; always wrapped results in `Promise.resolve()`.
- **Promise migration (2014-10):** switched the implementation to promises; moved from Q to Bluebird (2014-06).
- **Error handling:** improved the error message when a non-yieldable is yielded.
- **Quality and tooling:** added tests (recursion, "already running"), added test coverage, enabled Travis CI and io.js testing, removed an unused dependency.
- **Releases:** version-bump commits 3.0.1 to 4.6.0.
- **Documentation:** README and History.md updates, badges.

**Things to check before you attach it:**

## How this can be verified
The commit SHAs are cryptographic identifiers of the repository content. The client, or anyone with authorised access to the repo, can confirm each one with:

    git show --stat <sha>
